| P2 | Ch Chapter 9 (CH9-04, p.Section 5, Common Mistakes, fifth bullet (protocol overhead)) | The claim that 200/400GbE 'transcodes 64b/66b to 256b/257b before FEC' needs verification against IEEE 802.3. The PCS layer for 200GBASE-R/400GBASE-R uses 64b/66b encoding; the subsequent FEC (typically RS(544,514) / KP4) operates on different block structures. The phrase 'transcodes to 256b/257b' implies a direct encoding conversion that may not accurately describe the standard's layering. Additionally, 200GbE and 400GbE may use different lane counts and FEC configurations, making the lumped '200/400GbE' claim potentially imprecise. | chapter-09.md:144 quote ✓ and tag ✓, but the mechanism is real: an IEEE 802.3bs contribution analyzes '64/66B-256/257B transcoding' in 400GbE before FEC (ieee802.org/3/bs). | No change needed; optionally cite the IEEE 802.3bs transcoding contribution to make the claim verifiable. | REJECTED | Rejected at verification: chapter-09.md:144 quote ✓ and tag ✓, but the mechanism is real: an IEEE 802.3bs contribution analyzes '64/66B-256/257B transcoding' in 400GbE before FEC (ieee802.org/3/bs). |

If the offered rate does not grow with the user population, the load on the fleet is unchanged, and the hypothesis is trivially satisfied regardless of user count. The user-population growth is cosmetically present in the hypothesis statement but operationally neutralized by the assumptions.
